# SOTI Snap help — Add a User List Widget
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/add_a_user_list_widget
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/add_a_user_list_widget

### Add a User List Widget

#### Before you begin

The User List widget is only available for workflow applications.

#### About this task

Use the User List widget to create a filtered list of users or user groups from which the app user can select. The typical application for the User List widget is to select authorized approvers in workflow applications. Note: The list of available users are from SOTI Identity and includes any integrated third-party identity providers (IDPs).

#### Procedure

- In the Canvas , open the Widgets tab.

- Drag the User List widget onto the app canvas.

- In the User List Properties panel, enter an ID and Name for the widget or keep the defaults.

- Optional: Use the Display text field to give hint text indicating the type of content to enter in the text box. The display text disappears when the user makes a selection.

- In General , set the following options: | | Option | Description
| Required | Select to require the user to make a selection from the list.
| Allow Multiple Value Selection | Select to allow the user to select more than one list item.
| Preserve The Last Submitted Value
| Select to offer the user's last selection each time the user browses the list.

- In Users & Groups , set the following options to decide the users and groups that the app user may select: Note: You must specify at least one user or group.
| | Option | Description
| Allow All Users & Groups
| Options:
- Turn on to make all users and groups selectable.

- Turn off to set specific users or groups as selectable.
| List Of Users Which Can Be Selected | Select the users to populate the user list.
| List Of Groups Which Can Be Selected
| Select the user groups to populate the user list.

- In the Widget Alignment section, choose how you want to align your widget in the app.
- Align Left

- Center

- Align Right

- Optional: In the Rules section, add a requirement rule to the widget. Use requirement rules to specify whether the app user must configure this widget or can skip it.

- Select Requirement Rule to open the Set Requirement Rule dialog box.

- Select whether the requirement rule requires meeting all or any conditions.

- From the Field list, select an app field or variable and an operator. Enter the value required for the widget to be visible.

- Select Add Condition to add another condition.

- Select OK to save.

- Optional: In the Rules section, add a visibility rule to the widget. Use visibility rules to control when a widget is visible to the app user. For example, you can create a condition that a widget should only appear after the app user fills in a field or submits a form.

- Select Visibility Rule to open the Set Visibility Rule dialog box.

- Select whether the visibility rule requires meeting all or any conditions.

- Select an app field or variable and an operator from the Field list. Enter the value required for the widget to be visible.

- Select Add Condition to add another condition.

- Select Save .

- Move to the Format tab to customize the widget's appearance.

- Optional: Add events and actions to the widget. Events are activities that you can set to trigger an Action. For example, you can configure a widget to navigate to a new section in the app when the user selects it. Important: Events and actions offer a wide range of combinations. The following steps are guidelines only. Replace the listed events and actions with ones that suit your app's needs and structure. Learn more about available events and actions at Events and Actions .

- Move to the Events tab.

- Choose an event such as On Select/Deselect from the Events list, then select Add beside Actions .

- Select an action such as Run Blockly and configure the action. Select Add to apply it to the widget.

- Select Save to save the action.

- Repeat for more event and action combinations and return to the Widget Properties tab.

- Select Save in the SOTI Snap banner to apply your changes.

#### Results

You have configured your User List widget to show specific users or groups and set formatting and events. Tip: Select Preview to see how the widget appears in your app.

#### What to do next

Connecting a User List Widget to a Workflow Activity

Related information
- Connecting a User List Widget to a Workflow Activity

- Available Widgets

- Using Widgets

# SOTI Snap help — Work with Records in a Table Widget
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/add_or_edit_records_with_a_table_widget
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/add_or_edit_records_with_a_table_widget

### Work with Records in a Table Widget

The Table widget supports create, read, update, and delete (CRUD) operations on the data. The following sections explain how to update table data. Note: Data cannot be edited within the table. To add or edit table data, establish the widgets that will edit row data on behalf of the table first. See Prepare Widgets for Record Editing .

- Add Records

- Edit Records

- Delete Records

# SOTI Snap help — Add Records
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/add_records
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/add_records

### Add Records

#### Before you begin

App users cannot add data directly to the table. Ensure that you have linked appropriate widgets to enter the table row data for addition to the table. See Add Rows to a Table Using a Table Block and Prepare Widgets for Record Editing .

#### Modify the Page Controls

##### About this task

In this exercise, we will create an Add User button to navigate to the record editing page. On the record editing page, we will create another Add User button to save the new record.

##### Procedure

- In the Canvas , open the Widgets tab.

- From the list of forms and pages, select the page with the table. Double-click or drag and drop a Button widget and place it below the table on the form.

- In the Button Properties tab, go to the Display Text field and enter Add User.

- Click the button's Events tab. Under the Events dropdown, select On Click .

- Below the Events dropdown, click the Add button. The Add Action pop-up will open.

- From the Actions dropdown, select Navigate. From the Link To dropdown, select the name of the record editing page. Click Add .

- From the list of forms and pages, select the record editing page. Double-click or drag and drop a Button widget and place it below the table on the form.

- In the Button Properties tab, go to the Display Text field and enter Add User.

#### Prepare the Blockly Code

##### Procedure

- For the Add User button, click the Events tab. Under the Events dropdown, select On Click .

- Below the Events dropdown, click the Add button. The Add Action pop-up will open.

- From the Actions dropdown, select Run Blockly. Click Blockly Editor , and the Blockly editor will open.

- In the Blockly Name field, enter a name for the Blockly script.

- In the Blockly editor's left-hand panel, click the Connections group and drag a connection block into the main area.

- In the REST dropdown, select POST to allow new data to be added to the data source. In the URL block, enter the address of the database API (Application Programming Interface) connection.

- To update the data on the table after an edit is committed, you must request a refresh. Click the Table group, and drag the refresh table block to the on success section of the connection block. From the refresh table dropdown, select the name of the table to be refreshed.

- Once the table is updated, you will want the app to navigate to the table page to see the results. Click the App Actions group, and drag the navigate to block below the refresh table block. From the navigate to dropdown, select the name of the table page.

- To link source data with the edited fields, perform the following:
- Click the Data button. Beside the JSON dropdown, click the Add button.

- In the key text block (white quote bubble), enter the name of the first data field as identified in your data source.

- Click the Widget Values block group, and drag the get textboxes block to the value block.

- In the get textboxes dropdown, select the name of the widget that will hold the edited value of the first data field.

- Click the Add button, and repeat this step for all edited fields.

- Click Save . You will return to the Canvas .

- Select Save in the SOTI Snap banner to apply your changes.

#### Test the Blockly Code

##### About this task

To test your Blockly code, perform the following:

##### Procedure

- In the Canvas , click the preview button to run the app. The Preview Options window will open.

- Click Preview to accept the default skin. The app will open.

- On the table page, click the Add User button. The record editing page will open.

- On the record editing page, enter the first and last names of a new user. Click Add User to save the record and return to the table page.

- Review the table to ensure the new record is present.

##### Results

Changes to records should be reflected in the table's row data. If not, check the steps taken under Prepare Widgets for Record Editing , Modify the Page Controls , and Prepare the Blockly Code above.

# SOTI Snap help — Add a Chart Widget
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/adding_a_chart_widget
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/adding_a_chart_widget

### Add a Chart Widget

#### About this task

Use the Chart widget to display a visual relationship between data.

#### Procedure

- In the Canvas , open the Widgets tab.

- Double-click or drag and drop the Chart widget onto the app canvas.

The Chart Properties panel displays on the right with its sections.

- In the Chart section, set the following fields:
- Enter an ID and Name for the widget or keep the defaults.

- In the Configure Chart section, select Configure Chart to launch the Configure Chart dialog box.

- Open the Chart Formatting.

- For the Type option, select the chart type
- Column Chart

- Bar Chart

- Pie Chart

- Line Chart

- For the Title field, enter a title for the chart type. You can also select Aa to apply Title Formatting .

- Toggle the Data Label checkbox to present more details for each of the data values.
- Column/Bar/Line Chart: Display the exact numerical values for each data point/bar.

- Pie Chart: For each of the pie chart slice, display the slice's category next to the slice.

- Toggle the Legend checkbox to present more details for the entire set of data.
- Column/Bar/Line Chart: At the bottom of the chart, display each of the selected Series field.

- Pie Chart: At the bottom of the chart, display all possible values for the Series field.

- For the Source Selection selection, select the source of the data used by the chart. Choose from:
- Rest API : This is the default selection, with Default API (Test Data) chosen. Select to add more REST API connections. Restriction: If you choose to later toggle Save Chart To Report , you cannot submit a chart using the Default API (Test Data) to the SOTI Snap report. SOTI Snap uses the default Default API (Test Data) for testing only.

- Data Source

- Form Fields: SOTI Snap builds the chart based on the submitted values of all device users that has access to the chart-widget-using app, not just the current device user.

- For the Label field, select the data field that represents the independent variables aspect of the chart. You can select both number-based and character-based data fields.

- For the Series field, select the data field that represents the dependent variables of the chart. You can only select number-based data fields. Restriction: For non-Pie Charts, you can select up to 5 Series . For Pie Charts, you may only select 1 Series max.

- Toggle Data Grouping to group identical Label data values together to cluster and transform the Series field 's value with the following aggregate functions:
- Sum

- Count

- Average

- Toggle Plot As Distinct Values to transform the Series field 's value without any clustering and display each of the constituent Series values Restriction:
- This option is only available after you enabled Data Grouping .

- This option is not available for Pie Charts.

- For the Series Title field, enter a title for the Series field.

- Use Data Filtering to dynamically transform the entire chart to see only a subset of all data values. Select to add new filtering rules to extract the exact subset you want.

- Select Save to finish configuring the chart.

- In the Export section, set the following fields:
- Toggle Save Chart To Report to save the chart as part of the SOTI Snap report. Access the report from Using Report Viewer .

- In the Data Sync section, set the following fields:
- Toggle Auto Refresh to automatically refresh the chart with any updated values of Source Selection 's selected data source. You must enter an Auto Refresh Timing (Sec) with a value of at least 1 second. Restriction: The Auto Refresh toggle is not applicable if you selected a Data Source for Source Selection . To refresh a chart using a Data Source , you must instead manually import the data source file again or establish a new connection with Cloud-based data source file again.

- Optional: In the Rules section, add a visibility rule to the widget. Use visibility rules to control when a widget is visible to the app user. For example, you can create a condition that a widget should only appear after the app user fills in a field or submits a form.

- Select Visibility Rule to open the Set Visibility Rule dialog box.

- Select whether the visibility rule requires meeting all or any conditions.

- Select an app field or variable and an operator from the Field list. Enter the value required for the widget to be visible.

- Select Add Condition to add another condition.

- Select Save .

- Move to the Format tab to customize the widget's appearance.

- Select Save in the SOTI Snap banner to apply your changes.

#### Results

Tip: Select Preview to see how the widget appears in your app.

Related information
- Available Widgets

# SOTI Snap help — Adding a Repeater Widget
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/adding_a_repeater_widget
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/adding_a_repeater_widget

### Adding a Repeater Widget

#### About this task

Use the Repeater widget to enable your device users to submit multiple entries within a single record.

#### Procedure

- In the Canvas , open the Widgets tab.

- Double-click or drag the Repeater widget onto the app canvas.

The Repeater Properties panel displays on the right with its sections.

- From the Widgets Tab, you can drag other widgets inside the Repeater widget.

- In the General section, set the following fields:
- Enter an ID and Name for the widget or keep the defaults.

- In the Repeater Options section, set the following fields:
- For the Max amount of entries field, enter the number of maximum entries a device user may enter. You may leave this field blank to represent unlimited entries.

- In the Repeater Pagination section, set the following fields:
- For the Styles field, select the appearance of the pagination user interface.

- For the Position field, select the location of the pagination user interface. Choose from:
- Top

- Bottom

- Both

- In the Background section, set the following fields:
- For the Fill field, select whether you want to use a Color or Image for the background.
- Color: From the Color Scale , enter a color code or a select a color.

- Image: Upload Image from your device and then select a Scale . Choose from:
- Fit

- Fill

- Stretch

- Tile

- Center

- In the Widget Alignment section, choose how you want to align your widget in the app.
- Align Left

- Center

- Align Right

- Optional: In the Rules section, add a visibility rule to the widget. Use visibility rules to control when a widget is visible to the app user. For example, you can create a condition that a widget should only appear after the app user fills in a field or submits a form.

- Select Visibility Rule to open the Set Visibility Rule dialog box.

- Select whether the visibility rule requires meeting all or any conditions.

- Select an app field or variable and an operator from the Field list. Enter the value required for the widget to be visible.

- Select Add Condition to add another condition.

- Select Save .

- Optional: In the Miscellaneous section, decide which name you want to appear in the widget report. The Same as widget name setting is the default selection and automatically uses the widget name as the chart title. To choose your own chart title, clear the option and enter a new name. You may use special characters except for % & < > { } * | ^ and double spaces.

- Move to the Format tab to customize the widget's appearance.

- Optional: Add events and actions to the widget. Events are activities that you can set to trigger an Action. For example, you can configure a widget to navigate to a new section in the app when the user selects it. Important: Events and actions offer a wide range of combinations. The following steps are guidelines only. Replace the listed events and actions with ones that suit your app's needs and structure. Learn more about available events and actions at Events and Actions .

- Move to the Events tab.

- Choose one of the following events from the Events list:
- On Repeater Load : Triggers when SOTI Snap Device Agent loads and initializes the repeater widget.

- On Repeater Update : Triggers every time the device user successfully does one of the following
- Add a new entry and SOTI Snap Device Agent renders the new entry. The current entry is null.

- Update and Save an existing entry, then SOTI Snap Device Agent renders the next entry. The current entry is next one after the Save .

- Delete an existing entry and SOTI Snap Device Agent renders the next entry. The current entry is next one after the Delete .

- On Entry Render : Triggers when the device user renders an existing entry using pagination arrows or Back / Next . The current entry is the rendered entry.

- On Before Entry Save : Triggers just after the device user successfully selects Add or Save and just before the user interface switches to the new/updated entry. The current entry is the entry the device user is trying to add/save.

- On Before Entry Delete : Triggers when the device user attempts to delete the entry regardless of whether the user confirmed the deletion or not. The current entry is the entry the device user is trying to delete.

- On New Entry Display : Triggers after the user interfaces switches a new entry when the device user selects Add .

- Select Add beside Actions .

- Select an action such as Run Blockly and configure the action. Select Add to apply it to the widget.

- Select Save to save the action.

- Repeat for more event and action combinations and return to the Widget Properties tab.

- Select Save in the SOTI Snap banner to apply your changes.

#### Results

Tip: Select Preview to see how the widget appears in your app.

Related information
- Available Widgets

# SOTI Snap help — Adding a Salesforce Data Connection
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/adding_a_salesforce_data_method
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/adding_a_salesforce_data_method

### Adding a Salesforce Data Connection

#### Before you begin

- Sign in as a SOTI Snap user with the Apps permission enabled. See Users and Permissions for more details.

- You must have a Salesforce account with the appropriate environment access (Sandbox or Production) to link with SOTI Snap .

#### About this task

Use this procedure to add a Salesforce data connection to a Table widget in SOTI Snap using the REST API data method. Connect your table to an external data source so users can view, search, and update live records directly in the app. This integration removes the need to switch between applications and helps administrators consistently track and manage data across the organization.

#### Procedure

- From the SOTI Snap menu, create a new app or select an existing app to open the canvas.

- Add a Table widget or select an existing Table widget to open the properties panel.

- In the widget properties panel, navigate to the Get Data Method section.

- Expand Get Data Method and select Data connection .

- In the Choose Data Connection panel, select (add) to create a new data connection.

- In the Create REST API window, enter a name in the Name field and select Salesforce as the destination.

- In the Add Connection window, complete the following:

- Enter a name in the Connection Name field.

- Select from either the Production or Sandbox environment type from the Environment Type dropdown.

- Select Sign in with Salesforce to complete the authentication process in Salesforce and select the lists to be imported into SOTI Snap .

- Select Save .

- Select the Salesforce data connection under the Choose Data Connection dropdown in the widget properties menu. Important: When linking table columns to checkbox, dropdown, or radio button widgets, the options in the linked widget must match the options in the Salesforce data. Otherwise, data loss may occur upon form submission.

#### Results

The Salesforce data connection has been created in SOTI Snap .

#### What to do next

Verify in the SOTI Snap app that users can view and modify data within the table to confirm that the data connection has been successfully connected.

Related information
- Building A Standard App

- Using Widgets

- Data Methods for Table Widgets

# SOTI Snap help — Adding a SharePoint List Data Connection
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/adding_a_sharepoint_list_data_method
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/adding_a_sharepoint_list_data_method

### Adding a SharePoint List Data Connection

#### Before you begin

- Sign in as a SOTI Snap user with the Apps permission enabled. See Users and Permissions for more details.

- You must have a Microsoft SharePoint account with the appropriate permissions to link with SOTI Snap .

#### About this task

Use this procedure to add a SharePoint List data connection to a Table widget in SOTI Snap using the REST API data method. Connect your table to an external data source so users can view, search, and update live records directly in the app. This integration removes the need to switch between applications and helps administrators consistently track and manage data across the organization.

#### Procedure

- From the SOTI Snap menu, create a new app or select an existing app to open the canvas.

- Add a Table widget or select an existing Table widget to open the properties panel.

- In the widget properties panel, navigate to the Get Data Method section.

- Expand Get Data Method and select Data connection .

- In the Choose Data Connection panel, select (add) to create a new data connection.

- In the Create REST API window, enter a name and select SharePoint List as the destination.

- In the Create SharePoint Account window, complete the following:

- Provide a Name for the data connection.

- Select Authenticate to authenticate with Microsoft and select from any created SharePoint Lists.

- Select Save .

- Select the SharePoint List under the Choose Data Connection dropdown in the widget properties menu. Important: When linking table columns to checkbox, dropdown, or radio button widgets, the options in the linked widget must match the options in the SharePoint data. Otherwise, data loss may occur upon form submission.

#### Results

The SharePoint list has been created and selected for use in the table widget in a SOTI Snap app.

#### What to do next

Verify in the SOTI Snap app that users can view and modify data within the table to confirm that the data connection has been successfully connected.

Related information
- Building A Standard App

- Using Widgets

- Data Methods for Table Widgets

# SOTI Snap help — Add a Timer Widget
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/adding_a_timer_widget
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/adding_a_timer_widget

### Add a Timer Widget

#### Before you begin

Remember: The timer does not start by itself. You must add at least one widget (such as a button ) specifically used for managing the timer.

#### About this task

Use the Timer widget if you need a countdown timer or a stop watch for your app's functionality. To add a timer widget:

#### Procedure

- In the Canvas , open the Widgets tab.

- Double-click or drag the Timer widget onto the app canvas.

The Timer Properties panel displays on the right with its sections.

- In the General section, set the following fields:
- Enter an ID and Name for the widget or keep the defaults.

- In the Timer Properties section, set the following fields:
- For Timer Mode , select the type of timer you want the timer to be. Choose from a Stopwatch timer or a Countdown timer.

- For Starting Time , enter the starting time of the timer. The format is ( hh:mm:ss )
- Stopwatch : The default is 00:00:00 .

- Countdown : The default is 00:00:01 . Restriction: The starting time for Countdown must be 00:00:01 or greater.

- For Timer Mode , select the time format you want the device user to see. Choose from:
- 35 (Seconds Only)

- 12:35 (minutes, seconds)

- 4:12:35 (hours, minutes, seconds)

- Custom: Enter any string you want. The string should contain at least one of the following Macros to represent the time:
- %s% or %m% or %h% : The second/minute/hour without leading zeroes.

- %ss% or %mm% or %hh% : The second/minute/hour in exactly two digit only.

- %sss% or %mmm% or %hhh% : The total amount of second/minute/hours.

- Optional: Toggle Is Visible to show/hide timer to the device user.

- Optional: Toggle Show Timer Icon to show/hide the timer icon.

- In the Text Formatting section, change the default formatting settings for text.

- In the Widget Alignment section, choose how you want to align your widget in the app.
- Align Left

- Center

- Align Right

- Optional: In the Rules section, add a visibility rule to the widget. Use visibility rules to control when a widget is visible to the app user. For example, you can create a condition that a widget should only appear after the app user fills in a field or submits a form.

- Select Visibility Rule to open the Set Visibility Rule dialog box.

- Select whether the visibility rule requires meeting all or any conditions.

- Select an app field or variable and an operator from the Field list. Enter the value required for the widget to be visible.

- Select Add Condition to add another condition.

- Select Save .

- Optional: In the Miscellaneous section, decide which name you want to appear in the widget report. The Same as widget name setting is the default selection and automatically uses the widget name as the chart title. To choose your own chart title, clear the option and enter a new name. You may use special characters except for % & < > { } * | ^ and double spaces.

- Move to the Format tab to customize the widget's appearance.

- Optional: Add events and actions to the widget. Events are activities that you can set to trigger an Action. For example, you can configure a widget to navigate to a new section in the app when the user selects it. Important: Events and actions offer a wide range of combinations. The following steps are guidelines only. Replace the listed events and actions with ones that suit your app's needs and structure. Learn more about available events and actions at Events and Actions .

- Move to the Events tab.

- Choose one of the following events from the Events list:
- On Timer Ended : Selected by default. Triggers when the timer reaches 00:00:00 . Restriction: This event triggers for the countdown mode only even though it is available for stopwatch mode.

- On Timer Ticked : Triggers once every entered Interval of time. For example, if you set 00:00:10 , it triggers once every 10 seconds.

- On Timer Reached : Triggers once for each of the timestamps(s) you have added. By default, under the Time list, there is initially only one time stamp. You can select to add and edit another time stamp. Switch between customizing different timestamps by selecting the radio-button to the left of a time stamp. Tip: You may have different actions for each time stamp. Likewise, you may have more than one event for the same time stamp.

- Select Add beside Actions .

- Select an action such as Run Blockly and configure the action. Select Add to apply it to the widget.

- Select Save to save the action.

- Repeat for more event and action combinations and return to the Widget Properties tab.

- For the other Widget(s) used for managing the timer, configure its Events and Actions with Timer Actions .

- Select Save in the SOTI Snap banner to apply your changes.

#### Results

Tip: Select Preview to see how the widget appears in your app.

Related information
- Available Widgets

# SOTI Snap help — Add a Barcode Widget
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/barcode_widget
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/barcode_widget

### Add a Barcode Widget

#### About this task

Use the Barcode widget to request the device's camera to scan and read information delivered through a barcode.

#### Procedure

- In the Canvas , open the Widgets tab.

- Double-click or drag and drop the Barcode widget onto the app canvas. The Barcode Properties panel displays its sections on the right.

- In the General section, set the following fields:
- Enter an ID and Barcode Name for the widget or keep the defaults.

- Optional: Enable the Field Label toggle if you like to enter a custom label for this widget using the Text field box.

- Optional: Use the Display text field to give hint text indicating the type of content to enter in the text box. The display text disappears when the user makes a selection.

- In the Scan Setting section, set the following fields:
- Turn on Save Scanned Value to save barcode values in the Barcode column in reports.

- Turn on Overwrite to overwrite previous values with the latest scanned value.

- Turn on Allow Continuous Scanning to enable the app user to scan more than one barcode consecutively. If you turn this option off, the app user must relaunch the barcode widget to scan each barcode.

- Select the barcode formats you want to enable. The app ignores unselected barcode formats. This option can improve speed and performance by limiting the number of barcode formats to only those that your device app users may encounter.

- For Scan Device , choose which photo-capturing hardware to use when taking a picture of the barcode.
- Device Camera : Use the device's default built-in camera.

- Specialized Device : Use a specialized scanner provided by the device's vendor. From the Select Vendor drop-down menu, choose the device's vendor. If there is an issue with the vendor's specialized scanner, you can Use Device's Camera In Case Of Any Issues With Device Scanner instead. Remember: When you select Specialized Device , your device users must download a different SOTI Snap Device Agent specific to the vendor you have chosen.
- Zebra-specific SOTI Snap Device Agent

- In the Text Formatting section, change the default formatting settings for text.

- In the Widget Alignment section, choose how you want to align your widget in the app.
- Align Left

- Center

- Align Right

- Optional: In the Rules section, add a requirement rule to the widget. Use requirement rules to specify whether the app user must configure this widget or can skip it.

- Select Requirement Rule to open the Set Requirement Rule dialog box.

- Select whether the requirement rule requires meeting all or any conditions.

- From the Field list, select an app field or variable and an operator. Enter the value required for the widget to be visible.

- Select Add Condition to add another condition.

- Select OK to save.

- Optional: In the Rules section, add a visibility rule to the widget. Use visibility rules to control when a widget is visible to the app user. For example, you can create a condition that a widget should only appear after the app user fills in a field or submits a form.

- Select Visibility Rule to open the Set Visibility Rule dialog box.

- Select whether the visibility rule requires meeting all or any conditions.

- Select an app field or variable and an operator from the Field list. Enter the value required for the widget to be visible.

- Select Add Condition to add another condition.

- Select Save .

- Optional: In the Miscellaneous section, decide which name you want to appear in the widget report. The Same as widget name setting is the default selection and automatically uses the widget name as the chart title. To choose your own chart title, clear the option and enter a new name. You may use special characters except for % & < > { } * | ^ and double spaces.

- Move to the Format tab to customize the widget's appearance.

- Optional: Add an OnScan event and applicable actions to the widget. Events are activities that you can set to trigger an Action. When the app user scans a barcode or QR code, SOTI Snap triggers the OnScan event. Important: Due to the complex combinations available to use, the following steps are guidelines only. You'll need to replace the listed events and actions with your own to suit your app's needs. Learn more about the available events and actions at Events and Actions .

- Switch to the Events tab.

- Choose the OnScan event from the Events dropdown list and then select Add beside Actions .

- Select the Map Scanned Value action and configure it. Note: You must configure the Map Scanned Value action before you configure any other actions.

- Select Add to apply it to the widget.

- Select Save to save the action.

- Repeat for any other event and action combinations and return to the Properties tab.

- Select Save in the SOTI Snap banner to apply your changes.

#### Results

Tip: Select Preview to see how the widget appears in your app.

Related information
- Map Scanned Value

- Available Widgets

# SOTI Snap help — Add a Check Box Widget
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/checkbox_widget
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/checkbox_widget

### Add a Check Box Widget

#### About this task

Use the Check Box widget to add a set of check boxes to your app.
Note: App users can select multiple options from the check box widget. If you want app users to be able to only select one of the provided options, use the Radio Button widget instead.

#### Procedure

- In the Canvas , open the Widgets tab.

- Double-click or drag and drop the Check Box widget onto the app canvas.

- In the Check Box Properties panel, enter a name for the widget in the Name field.

- Enable the Required check box if you want to force app users to select at least one of the options before they can submit the form.

- Enter display text for each of the options. Click Add beside an option to add a new option.

- Choose how you want your options to be aligned.
- Auto-wrap text

- Set Column – Defines the number of columns for options. This is only available if Auto-wrap is not selected.

- Align Top – Aligns text with the top of the option

- Align Center – Aligns text with the center of the option

- Horizontal spacing between options

- Vertical spacing between options

- Choose how the records for this widget should appear when exported to reports or using data export connections. To let the external source decide them automatically, leave Default selected. If you want to customize how they appear, clear Default and update the Data settings: Format field to your preferred format.
- Expand the dropdown icon above the right corner of the field to enter macros that represent the data in each display column. Double percentage signs encase macros: %%macro%% . You cannot manually enter a macro.

- Enter text characters.

- Repeat to add more macros or text characters.

Do not leave the Data settings: Format field in the middle of creating your format. You could lose your work because the field reverts to the last valid format whenever you leave the field.

- In the Text Formatting section, change the default formatting settings for text.

- In the Widget Alignment section, choose how you want to align your widget in the app.
- Align Left

- Center

- Align Right

- Optional: In the Rules section, add a requirement rule to the widget. Use requirement rules to specify whether the app user must configure this widget or can skip it.

- Select Requirement Rule to open the Set Requirement Rule dialog box.

- Select whether the requirement rule requires meeting all or any conditions.

- From the Field list, select an app field or variable and an operator. Enter the value required for the widget to be visible.

- Select Add Condition to add another condition.

- Select OK to save.

- Optional: In the Rules section, add a visibility rule to the widget. Use visibility rules to control when a widget is visible to the app user. For example, you can create a condition that a widget should only appear after the app user fills in a field or submits a form.

- Select Visibility Rule to open the Set Visibility Rule dialog box.

- Select whether the visibility rule requires meeting all or any conditions.

- Select an app field or variable and an operator from the Field list. Enter the value required for the widget to be visible.

- Select Add Condition to add another condition.

- Select Save .

- Optional: In the Miscellaneous section, decide which name you want to appear in the widget report. The Same as widget name setting is the default selection and automatically uses the widget name as the chart title. To choose your own chart title, clear the option and enter a new name. You may use special characters except for % & < > { } * | ^ and double spaces.

- Move to the Format tab to customize the widget's appearance.

- Optional: Add events and actions to the widget. Events are activities that you can set to trigger an Action. For example, you can configure a widget to navigate to a new section in the app when the user selects it. Important: Events and actions offer a wide range of combinations. The following steps are guidelines only. Replace the listed events and actions with ones that suit your app's needs and structure. Learn more about available events and actions at Events and Actions .

- Move to the Events tab.

- Choose an event such as On Click from the Events list and then select Add beside Actions .

- Select an action such as Navigate and configure the action. Select Add to apply it to the widget.

- Select Save to save the action.

- Repeat for any more event and action combinations and return to the Widget Properties tab.

- Select Save in the SOTI Snap banner to apply your changes.

#### Results

Tip: Select Preview to see how the widget appears in your app.

# SOTI Snap help — Configuring Global Variables for Widgets
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/configuring_global_variables
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/configuring_global_variables

### Configuring Global Variables for Widgets

#### Before you begin

- You must have one or more Global Variables created in SOTI Snap . See Global Variables for more information.

- Log in as a SOTI Snap user with the Apps permissions enabled. See Users and Permissions for more details.

#### About this task

Use this procedure to configure Global Variables in SOTI Snap . Dynamic values enables the Label and Paragraph widgets to display created Global Variables , eliminating the need to manually configure values and enabling more context-aware app workflows.

#### Procedure

- From the SOTI Snap menu, create or select an existing app to open the canvas.

- Add or select a Label or Paragraph widget to open the Label Properties panel.

- In Label Properties , select beside Display Text to open the Set dynamic values window.

- From the Set dynamic values window, select Global Variables .

- Select a created Global Variable . See Global Variables for more information on adding new Global Variables.

- Select Done .

#### Results

The dynamic values are automatically added to the Display Text field in the widget's Properties menu and appear on the app canvas.

#### What to do next

Publish the SOTI Snap app and verify that devices correctly show the selected values while the app is running. See Devices: Publishing and Distributing Apps for more information.

Related information
- Using Widgets

- Available Widgets

- Dynamic Values in Widgets

# SOTI Snap help — Configuring SOTI MobiControl Device Values
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/configuring_mobicontrol_device_values
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/configuring_mobicontrol_device_values

### Configuring SOTI MobiControl Device Values

#### Before you begin

- You must have a SOTI MobiControl instance integrated with SOTI Snap . See Integrating SOTI MobiControl with SOTI Snap for Lockdown for more details.

- Log in as a SOTI Snap user with the Apps permissions enabled. See Users and Permissions for more details.

#### About this task

Use this procedure to configure MobiControl Device dynamic values in SOTI Snap . Dynamic values enable the Label and Paragraph widgets to display real-time, device-specific information retrieved from SOTI MobiControl , such as device identifiers, user details, network information, and system properties. These values automatically update based on the device context, eliminating the need to manually configure values and enabling more context-aware app workflows.

#### Procedure

- From the SOTI Snap menu, create or select an existing app to open the canvas.

- Add or select a Label or Paragraph widget to open the Label Properties panel.

- In Label Properties , select beside Display Text to open the Set dynamic values window.

- In the Set dynamic values window, select MobiControl Device .

- Select Macros to insert predefined device values used within SOTI MobiControl . For more information on available macros, see Macros in SOTI MobiControl .

- Select Custom Attribute to define a device attribute. Enter an Attribute name that references a custom attribute configured in SOTI MobiControl . For more information, see Creating Custom Attributes .

- Select Custom Data to use device-specific custom data stored in SOTI MobiControl . Enter the Data name to reference assigned values. For more information, see Using Custom Data .

- Select Done .

#### Results

The dynamic values are automatically added to the Display Text field in the widget's Properties menu and appear on the app canvas.

#### What to do next

Publish the SOTI Snap app and verify that devices correctly show the selected values while the app is running. See Devices: Publishing and Distributing Apps for more information.

Related information
- Using Widgets

- Available Widgets

- Dynamic Values in Widgets

# SOTI Snap help — Dynamic Values in Widgets
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/configuring_mobicontrol_values_in_soti_snap_widgets
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/configuring_mobicontrol_values_in_soti_snap_widgets

### Dynamic Values in Widgets

The Dynamic Values feature in SOTI Snap enables users to insert device, user, or system-generated data from SOTI MobiControl or SOTI Snap into widgets within a SOTI Snap app. Instead of using static text, users can configure widgets to display real-time values such as device details, user information, or global variables. This helps create more personalized and context-aware app experiences.
Dynamic values are configured through a dedicated selection window accessible from the Display Text field when configuring a Label or Paragraph widget. Selecting opens a panel where you can browse and insert available variables.
After selecting a value, it is inserted into the text field and displays within the SOTI Snap app. This enables the widget to automatically update its content based on the current device state, user context, or configured variables.
The dynamic values panel is organized into the following tabs:

- MobiControl Device : Provides device-specific data such as Macros , Custom Attributes , and Custom Data . Use these values to display real-time device information in the app. See Configuring SOTI MobiControl Device Values for more information.

- MobiControl User : Provides user-specific data including Username , First Name , Last Name , and Email . These values are useful for personalizing content for the logged-in user. See Configuring SOTI MobiControl User Values for more information.

- Global Variables : Displays variables defined at the app level. These variables can be reused across multiple widgets and configurations to ensure consistency. See Configuring Global Variables for Widgets for more details.

Related information
- Building A Standard App

# SOTI Snap help — Configuring SOTI MobiControl User Values
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/configuring_soti_mobicontrol_user_values
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/configuring_soti_mobicontrol_user_values

### Configuring SOTI MobiControl User Values

#### Before you begin

- You must have a SOTI MobiControl instance integrated with SOTI Snap . See Integrating SOTI MobiControl with SOTI Snap for Lockdown for more details.

- Log in as a SOTI Snap user with the Apps permissions enabled. See Users and Permissions for more details.

#### About this task

Use this procedure to configure MobiControl User dynamic values in SOTI Snap . Dynamic values enables the Label and Paragraph widgets to display user details retrieved from SOTI MobiControl . These values automatically update based on the device context, eliminating the need to manually configure values and enabling more context-aware app workflows.

#### Procedure

- From the SOTI Snap menu, create or select an existing app to open the canvas.

- Add or select a Label or Paragraph widget to open the Label Properties panel.

- In Label Properties , select beside Display Text to open the Set dynamic values window.

- Under Set dynamic values , select MobiControl User .

- Select a macro from the following list to insert user details from SOTI MobiControl . For more information about assigning user details to devices, see Managing Users .
- Username : The user name associated with the device.

- First Name : The user’s first name.

- Last Name : The user’s last name.

- Email : The user’s email address.

- Select Done .

#### Results

The dynamic values are automatically added to the Display Text field in the widget's Properties menu and appear on the app canvas.

#### What to do next

Publish the SOTI Snap app and verify that devices correctly show the selected values while the app is running. See Devices: Publishing and Distributing Apps for more information.

# SOTI Snap help — Connecting a User List Widget to a Workflow Activity
Source: https://pulse.soti.net/support/soti-Snap/help/?V=2026.1&T=/apps/build/widgets/connect_a_user_list_widget_to_a_workflow_app_2
SOTI Snap · help documentation · apps/build/widgets/connect_a_user_list_widget_to_a_workflow_app_2

### Connecting a User List Widget to a Workflow Activity

#### Before you begin

This task requires that your project has a User List widget configured in the workflow project.

#### About this task

After configuring a User List widget in your app, assign it as a list of authorized approvers for a User Approver activity in your workflow.

#### Procedure

- From the left toolbar, select Project > Workflow .

- Select the User Approval activity.

- From the Properties tab, select the Gear icon, then the User List icon. Choose from a configured User List widget as the source of approver names for the User Approval activity.

- Select Add .

- Select Save in the SOTI Snap banner to apply your changes.

#### Results

Your User List widget provides the list of selectable approvers for the User Approver activity of your workflow.

Related information
- Add a User List Widget

- Adding a User Approval

- Building A Standard App
